# Handoff Contract — the one interface for handoffs BETWEEN THE TWO MACHINES

The single, standardized way work passes **between the Mini and the work laptop**. The unit is the
**machine**, not the agent: `mini` and `laptop`. *(Scoped 2026-08-25 — Ricardo: "this one is about
which machine it comes from. It doesn't matter which model it comes from or which model is going to
open it." He may open any item with Claude or Codex.)*

A handoff is a **durable, explicit briefing that survives session boundaries** — a clean drop-point
for "here's what the next session on the other machine needs to know," independent of any live
context. One contract, one location.

**Same-machine handoffs are a different problem and use a different convention.** Where the *harness*
is what matters — Claude ⇄ Codex on one machine, or session-to-session within one harness — see the
laptop's `~/phantom/docs/handoffs`. This contract does not govern those; it governs the machine
boundary, which is the one carrying the IP and privacy rules.

**Handoffs use `~/handoff/`. Do NOT invent ad-hoc locations** (no `~/Documents/Codex`, no
per-conversation scratch dirs).

**Exception — work-internal handoffs on the laptop:** Claude ⇄ Codex handoffs that stay *inside*
Phantom use the pre-existing `~/phantom/docs/handoffs` convention — work-only, unchanged, never
leaves the Phantom folder, no IP gate. That is out of scope for this contract. `~/handoff/` on the
laptop is for **cross-machine** work↔personal handoffs only. (This contract governs cross-boundary
and cross-harness handoffs everywhere else.)

## The machines
- **MINI** (personal): home of `~/personal-kb`. Everything here is personal. An **IP-clean zone.**
- **LAPTOP** (work): work content + employer IP (`~/phantom/work-kb`). **MDM-managed, work-monitored.**

## Folders (identical on every machine)
- `~/handoff/outbox/` — items THIS agent is sending.
- `~/handoff/inbox/` — items to process (addressed to an agent on this machine).
- `~/handoff/inbox/processed/` — handled items (archive).
- `~/handoff/CONTRACT.md` — this file.

## Item format
One markdown file per handoff, `YYYYMMDD-HHMM-<slug>.md`, beginning with a manifest:
```
---
from: <machine>               # mini | laptop — REQUIRED
to:   <machine>               # mini | laptop — REQUIRED
purpose: <one line>
for: <optional free-text — which session/task, e.g. "the session continuing the Sentinel build">
ip_clean: yes|n/a             # REQUIRED = yes on every laptop→mini item (see IP rules)
sanitized_by: <who/what ran the redaction, or n/a>
---
```

**⚠️ `from:`/`to:` NAME MACHINES ONLY — NEVER AN AGENT OR HARNESS (clarified 2026-08-25, Ricardo).**
Write `from: mini` / `to: laptop`, **not** `mini/claude` or `laptop/codex`. His reasoning: *"This one
is about which machine it comes from. It doesn't matter which model it comes from or which model is
going to [open it]"* — he may open any item with Claude **or** Codex, so naming a harness is fiction.
- **The machine pair is load-bearing and must always be present**: it is the direction, and the
  direction is what the IP rules and the control gate key on (`laptop → mini` triggers the IP gate;
  `mini → laptop` triggers the personal-data gate). An item missing either field is a gate error.
- **Do not put a machine or a recipient in the FILENAME** — it stays `YYYYMMDD-HHMM-<slug>.md`.
- **Whichever harness opens the inbox handles the item.** Any item in a machine's `inbox/` is for
  whoever opens it there, with any harness.

> **Not to be confused with the work-laptop-internal handoff convention** (`~/phantom/docs/handoffs`),
> where the *harness* is exactly what matters because the handoff is Claude ⇄ Codex **within** the
> laptop. That is a separate contract, out of scope here. `~/handoff/` is the **cross-machine** one:
> machines in the manifest, harnesses never.
Attachments go in a sibling folder `YYYYMMDD-HHMM-<slug>/` next to the `.md`.

## Routing (`~/handoff/` is the CROSS-MACHINE channel)
- **Mini → laptop, or laptop → mini:** write the item to your `outbox/` with the machine pair set,
  run the gate, then push to the peer's inbox:
  `scp -r ~/handoff/outbox/<item> <peer>:~/handoff/inbox/` (`ssh mini` / `ssh laptop`).
- **Same-machine, harness-to-harness handoffs do NOT belong here.** On the laptop they use
  `~/phantom/docs/handoffs` (work-internal, harness-addressed). On the Mini, a Claude ⇄ Codex handoff
  is one trust domain — if you use `~/handoff/` for it, set `from:`/`to:` to the same machine
  (`mini` → `mini`); the gate will scan for secrets only.
- **Filenames stay `YYYYMMDD-HHMM-<slug>.md` — never encode a machine or a recipient in the name.**
- Whoever opens an inbox processes what is there, then moves each item to `inbox/processed/`.

## Session behavior
At session start, if you're expecting a handoff, check `~/handoff/inbox/` — everything sitting there
is for this machine, and whichever harness opens it handles it. Act on them, then expire them (below).

## Handoffs are EPHEMERAL — expire them
A handoff is a short-lived message, not an archive. Default lifetime: **delete-on-read** — once the
intended recipient has acted on an item, expire it. Backstop TTL: **nothing in `~/handoff/` should
outlive ~1.5 days**; a genuinely multi-step ("living") handoff may persist a little, but not beyond that.
- **Expire = move to the Trash, NEVER hard-delete.** No `rm`. Move the item to the macOS Trash so
  it's recoverable; Ricardo empties the Trash periodically. From an agent:
  `osascript -e 'tell application "Finder" to delete POSIX file "'"$HOME"'/handoff/inbox/<item>"'`
- Durable things do NOT belong in `~/handoff/` — put living documents in the KB or a repo. Only
  transient messages live here. `CONTRACT.md` is the spec, not a handoff item; it stays.
- If you find stale items (older than ~1.5 days, read or not), Trash them as part of good hygiene.
- **Automated:** on both machines, launchd `com.ricardo.handoff-sweep` runs `~/bin/handoff-sweep` daily at 07:15
  (and at login). It moves anything in `inbox/processed/` older than ~1.5 days to the Trash; the log is `/tmp/handoff-sweep.log`.

## Control gate — run it before every cross-machine send
`~/handoff/handoff-gate.sh <item>` is a gitleaks-style scanner. It reads the item's **`from:`/`to:`
machine pair**, derives the direction, and scans accordingly. **A missing or unrecognised machine on
either side is a hard error** — the gate refuses rather than guessing, because guessing wrong means
scanning for the wrong class of leak. It scans for: secrets always; `laptop → mini` also checks for the
`ip_clean: yes` attestation + a machine-local proprietary-term denylist + internal URLs/ticket IDs;
`mini → laptop` checks for SSNs, card numbers, and sensitive-document keywords + a personal denylist.
**A cross-machine item must PASS the gate (exit 0) before it goes in `outbox/` / is scp'd.** Exit 2
means review — do not send until it's clean. Denylists live private + machine-local under
`~/.config/handoff-gate/` (never committed): `work-terms.txt` on the laptop, `personal-terms.txt` on the Mini.

## ⛔ IP RULES — the reason the boundary exists (cross-MACHINE only)

Cross-harness on the SAME machine is one trust domain — no IP gate, just use the manifest.
Cross-**machine** carries the rules:

### Laptop → Mini (work → personal): SANITIZE AT THE SOURCE
Only IP-clean, transferable, generic content may cross. The **sending laptop agent MUST sanitize
BEFORE** the item enters `~/handoff/outbox`. Raw work IP NEVER enters `~/handoff`.
- **Exclude:** employer/product/colleague names, metric/KPI names, internal tool names (dbt models,
  dashboards, Slack/Linear/warehouse specifics), thresholds/numbers, incident IDs, proprietary logic.
- Every laptop→mini item MUST carry `ip_clean: yes` + `sanitized_by:` and end with a **redaction
  self-check** (grepped for company/tool/metric names + stray numbers, confirmed clean).
- The **receiving Mini agent treats inbox as clean but still sanity-checks.** Anything that looks
  like raw IP → quarantine (do NOT commit to the KB), flag Ricardo. Trust, but verify.

### Mini → Laptop (personal → work): NO SENSITIVE PERSONAL DATA
The laptop is MDM-managed and work-monitored. Only task requests, prompts, code, and IP-clean
material may cross. **NEVER send** health, finance, immigration, family, passwords/secrets, or any
sensitive personal content.

*Established 2026-07-19. 2026-08-25: harness/agent dropped from `from:`/`to:` — this channel is addressed by MACHINE, never by model. Generalized same day to cover cross-harness handoffs. Transport: Mini↔laptop SSH.*

## ⚠️ Telemetry caveat (learned 2026-07-21, Sentinel)
The gate sanitizes CONTENT, not METADATA. Work-side agents run through employer AI
infrastructure where every prompt and output is logged (gateway, observability). A
work→personal request is therefore permanently recorded ON THE WORK SIDE, including what
was asked and for whom — no sanitization changes that. Rule: ask a work agent only what
you would be comfortable seeing read back in an audit, and prefer NOT to involve work
agents in personal projects at all, even for IP-clean output. Public sources or the
personal side's own knowledge are the default; the work boundary is for work.

The models point to charge changes at the interface edge and an exposed aromatic residue worth testing. The simple charge counts are about −2, −7, and −4 for the three parents. I’ll pair single substitutions with a combined variant for each parent; these will be hypotheses to test, not predicted affinity gains.

from Bio.PDB.MMCIF2Dict import MMCIF2Dict
from pathlib import Path
from collections import defaultdict
import numpy as np
sites={'fbcf76':[18,38,45],'c674f8':[68,79,93],'8f7b63':[5,7,53,62]}
for key,ps in sites.items():
 d=MMCIF2Dict(str(next(Path('results/pods/c3_glp1r/3_Ranked').glob('*'+key+'*.cif'))));ats=[]
 for i in range(len(d['_atom_site.id'])):
  g=lambda k:d['_atom_site.'+k][i]
  ats.append((g('auth_asym_id'),int(g('auth_seq_id')),g('auth_comp_id'),g('auth_atom_id'),g('type_symbol'),np.array([float(g('Cartn_'+q)) for q in 'xyz'])))
 print('\\n',key)
 for pos in ps:
  site=[a for a in ats if a[0]=='B' and a[1]==pos and a[3] not in ['N','CA','C','O']]
  print('SITE',pos)
  near=[]
  for a in site:
   for b in ats:
    if b[0]=='A':
     dist=float(np.linalg.norm(a[-1]-b[-1]));
     if dist<4.5:near.append((dist,a[3],f'{b[2]}{b[1]}:{b[3]}'))
  print('target closest',[(round(v,2),a,b) for v,a,b in sorted(near)[:10]])
  polar=[]
  for a in site:
   if a[4] not in ['N','O']:continue
   for b in ats:
    if b[0]=='B' and abs(b[1]-pos)>1 and b[4] in ['N','O']:
     dist=float(np.linalg.norm(a[-1]-b[-1]));
     if dist<4.1:polar.append((dist,a[3],f'{b[2]}{b[1]}:{b[3]}'))
  print('intrabinder polar',[(round(v,2),a,b) for v,a,b in sorted(polar)])

from Bio.PDB.MMCIF2Dict import MMCIF2Dict
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np
for key,positions in {'fbcf76':[26,38,51,55],'c674f8':[58,63,71],'8f7b63':[15,29,55]}.items():
 d=MMCIF2Dict(str(next(Path('results/pods/c3_glp1r/3_Ranked').glob('*'+key+'*.cif'))));atoms=[]
 for i in range(len(d['_atom_site.id'])):
  g=lambda k:d['_atom_site.'+k][i]
  atoms.append((g('auth_asym_id'),int(g('auth_seq_id')),g('auth_atom_id'),np.array([float(g('Cartn_'+q)) for q in 'xyz'])))
 print(key)
 for p in positions:
  a={a[2]:a[3] for a in atoms if a[0]=='B' and a[1]==p};cz=a['CZ'];v=cz-(a['CE1']+a['CE2'])/2;oh=cz+1.36*v/np.linalg.norm(v)
  near=sorted([(np.linalg.norm(oh-x[-1]),x[:3]) for x in atoms if not (x[0]=='B' and x[1]==p)],key=lambda t:t[0])[:4]
  print('F->Y',p,[(round(float(v),2),s) for v,s in near])
